1609	# Initialize global temporary directory.
1610	# NOTE: /usr/tmp is a legacy SysV alias for /var/tmp that exists only as a symlink on
1611	# some RPM distros; on Debian/Ubuntu/Kali it is absent, so clones/builds under it failed
1612	# with "Permission denied". Use $TMPDIR when set, else /var/tmp -- persistent and
1613	# disk-backed (good for large source builds, unlike RAM-backed /tmp) and world-writable
1614	# + sticky on every Debian-family system, so the invoking non-root user can create build
1615	# dirs here. Export GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR to override.
1616	GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR="${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-${TMPDIR:-/var/tmp}}"
1617	GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR="${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR%/}"
1618	# Initialize the global temporary directory base.
1619	# It MUST be writable by the invoking (non-root) user, because scripts clone/build here
1620	# as the user (then `make install` as root). Pitfalls this resolver defends against:
1621	#   * /usr/tmp is a legacy SysV alias n/Ubuntu/Kali; worse,
1622	#     an older revision of this file created it as root:root 0755 (unwritable), and a
1623	#     long-running menu may still EXPOnto child scripts.
1624	#   * So a stale/unwritable INHERITED value must be SKIPPED, not preserved -- using
1625	#     "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-...}" alone would keep the bad /usr/tmp.
1626	# Resolution order (first that is a WRITABLE dir, or can be created writable, wins):
1627	#   exported GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR, $TMPDIR, /var/tmp, /tmp. /var/tmp is preferred (persistent +
1628	#   disk-backed -> good for large source builds, unlike RAM-backed /tmp).
1629	__resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gvar_common() {
1630	    local c
1631	    for c in "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-}" "${TMPDIR:-}" /var/tmp /tmp; do
1632	        [ -z "$c" ] && continue
1633	        c="${c%/}"
1634	        if { [ -d "$c" ] || mkdir -p "$c" 2>/dev/null; } && [ -w "$c" ]; then
        ... [truncated 26570 chars]

102	ensure_php_symlink() {
103	    echo -e "${BLUE}$SCRIPT_INDEX Ensuring PHP ${PHP_VERSION} is the default php...${NC}"
104	
105	    if [ ! -x "$PHP_BIN" ]; then
106	        echo -e "${RED}$SCRIPT_INDEX PHP ${PHP_VERSION} binary not found at $PHP_BIN${NC}"
107	        echo -e "${YELLOW}$SCRIPT_INDEX Please run 31_ensure_php85_intelligent.sh first${NC}"
108	        return 1
109	    fi
110	
111	    # 1) /usr/bin/php via update-alternatives -- SAME priority as step 31 (shared
112	    #    PHP_ALT_PRIORITY) so re-running this step never lowers the default 31 set.
113	    #    No --remove-all (that would wipe other registered php alternatives).
114	    if [ ! -e "/usr/bin/php" ] || ! /usr/bin/php -v 2>/dev/null | grep -q "PHP ${PHP_VERSION}"; then
115	        $USE_SUDO update-alternatives --install /usr/bin/php php "$PHP_BIN" "$PHP_ALT_PRIORITY"
116	        $USE_SUDO update-alternatives --set php "$PHP_BIN"
117	        echo -e "${GREEN}$SCRIPT_INDEX PHP ${PHP_VERSION} set as default (priority ${PHP_ALT_PRIORITY})${NC}"
118	    else
119	        echo -e "${GREEN}$SCRIPT_INDEX PHP ${PHP_VERSION} already the default php${NC}"
120	    fi
121	
122	    # 2) The canonical entrypoint link that step 31 CREATES and step 34 CONSUMES.
123	    #    32 previously only checked /usr/bin/php while this contract
124	    #    link was broken. Keep it in sync here too.
125	    if [ "$(readlink -f "$TARGET_LINK_PATH" 2>/dev/null)" != "$(readlink -f "$PHP_BIN" 2>/dev/null)" ]; then
126	        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$(dirname "$TARGET_LINK_PATH")"
127	        $USE_SUDO ln -sf "$PHP_BIN" "$TARGET_LINK_PATH"
128	        echo -e "${GREEN}$SCRIPT_INDEX Linked $TARGET_LINK_PATH -> $PHP_BIN${NC}"
129	    fi

ONE NON-TRIVIAL POWERSHELL DIFFERENCE (flagged per your request): PowerShell has NO `< /dev/null` equivalent — the `<` input-redirection operator is RESERVED/unimplemented (the PS parser rejects `< $null` with "The '<' operator is reserved for future use"). I caught this with the PowerShell AST parser. The faithful equivalent for non-interactive stdin is to PIPE empty input: `$null | pnpm install --config.confirm-modules-purge=true`. Piping $null gives pnpm a non-TTY stdin, so it won't offer the interactive purge — same effect as `< /dev/null`. This is the only semantic adaptation; everything else is a 1:1 mirror.

VALIDATION:
- `bash -n start.sh` => clean (BASH_SYNTAX_OK)
- start.ps1 validated with PowerShell AST parser ([Parser]::ParseFile, no execution) => PS1_SYNTAX_OK, zero parse errors
Did not run pnpm/node/the app and did not commit, per constraints.

UNCERTAINTY: The `$null | pnpm ...` approach reliably makes stdin non-interactive (no TTY), which suppresses the prompt. I cannot runtime-confirm that pnpm 11.8 on Windows, given confirm-modules-purge=true + non-TTY stdin on a breaking-change tree, EXITS NONZERO (vs. silently no-op) — but that mirrors the documented Linux behavior and our handler treats a nonzero exit as "kept tree + warn", which is the safe outcome either way.

<teammate-message teammate_id="rev-venv" color="orange" summary="Adversarial venv review: 1 MUST-FIX, 1 SHOULD-FIX">
# Adversarial Review: Python Venv Rework

## Findings

**[MUST-FIX] Circularity trap in `setup_production_python_venv` on rebuild**  
File: `scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/13_ensure_python.sh:809, 815, 830–831`

After first run, `/usr/local/bin/python3` points to the venv. On rebuild, lines 809 and 815 use bare `python3` (which now resolves to the venv), and fallback lines 830–831 use bare `python3 -m venv`, creating a circular build where the venv interpreter tries to rebuild itself.

**Fix:** Change line 769 to capture `local system_python3_path="/usr/bin/python3"` at function entry (before any venv setup), then use `$system_python3_path` on lines 809, 815, 830–831 instead of bare `python3`.

---

**[SHOULD-FIX] Stale `pythonorigin` symlink on system Python upgrades**  
File: `scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/13_ensure_python.sh:428–431`

The `link_commands_to_venv()` function always rewrites `/usr/local/bin/pythonorigin` → `/usr/bin/python3`. If system Python changes location or `/usr/bin/python3` becomes a managed symlink (e.g., via `update-alternatives`), the backup target drifts. While `ln -sf` is idempotent, the backup itself becomes unreliable across runs.

**Fix:** Explicitly capture the system interpreter path once at script initialization and store it persistently, then reuse throughout. Alternatively, document that `pythonorigin` reflects the interpreter at *first venv setup*, not on subsequent upgrades.

---

## Passing Checks

✓ No defects in `venv_python_common.sh` — all command-echoes use `>&2` (stderr), safe for `$(...)` captures.  
✓ PEP 668 flags (`--break-system-packages/--no-user`) correctly applied only to system python, not venv (run_pip_install_realtime lines 313–315).  
✓ `link_commands_to_venv()` idempotency — uses `ln -sf` throughout, safe to re-run.  
✓ `ensure_venv_user_writable()` is idempotent — checks ownership before `chown`, no spam.  
✓ No bash syntax errors in either script.

745	        stanza="$(zcat "$deb_idx" 2>/dev/null | awk -v RS='' -v p="$lib" 'index($0 "\n", "Package: " p "\n")==1' | head -c 200000)"
746	        fname="$(printf '%s\n' "$stanza" | sed -n 's/^Filename: //p' | head -n1)"
747	        exp_sha="$(printf '%s\n' "$stanza" | sed -n 's/^SHA256: //p' | head -n1)"
748	        if [ -z "$fname" ]; then
749	            echo "[php-compat] NOTE: Debian ${php_codename} has no '${lib}' package; cannot shim it." >&2
750	            missing="$missing $lib"; continue
751	        fi
752	        out="$tmp/${lib}.deb"
753	        echo "[php-compat] Fetching ${lib} <- https://deb.debian.org/debian/${fname}" >&2
754	        if ! curl -fsSL -o "$out" "https://deb.debian.org/debian/${fname}"; then
755	            echo "[php-compat] WARNING: download failed for ${lib}." >&2
756	            missing="$missing $lib"; continue
757	        fi
758	        if [ -z "$exp_sha" ]; then
759	            echo "[php-compat] ERROR: no SHA256 in index for ${lib}; refusing to install unverified .deb." >&2
760	            missing="$missing $lib"; continue
761	        fi
762	        got_sha="$(sha256sum "$out" 2>/dev/null | awk '{print $1}')"
763	        if [ "$got_sha" != "$exp_sha" ]; then
764	            echo "[php-compat] ERROR: SHA256 mismatch for ${lib} (want ${exp_sha}, got ${got_sha}); discarding." >&2
765	            missing="$missing $lib"; continue
766	        fi
767	        debs="$debs $out"
768	    done
769	
770	    # 5) Install all verified compat libs at once (so any inter-deps resolve together),
771	    #    non-interactively, keeping existing configs and never letting -f install remove
772	    #    packages to "fix" the transaction. Diagnostics are NOT swallowed.

<teammate-message teammate_id="disc-autostart" color="green" summary="Autostart/desktop patterns + design intent discovered">
## Autostart/.desktop Patterns & Startup Design — Discovery Complete

Found all existing reusable patterns and design intent. Here's the inventory:

### 1. **Windows Shortcut Creation (Desktop + Startup Folder)**
- **Main helper**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pycore/pyutils/common/icon_generator.py`
  - `DesktopIconGenerator.create_shortcut()` (line 245): Core shortcut creation via `win32com.client`
  - Features: PNG→ICO conversion, idempotent checks (`_shortcut_needs_update`), AppUserModelID support
  - Returns: `.lnk` file path
- **Wrapper**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pycore/pyutils/desktop/universal_shortcut.py`
  - `ShortcutManager.create_shortcut()` (line 173): Unified interface
  - `ShortcutManager.ensure_shortcut()` (line 365): Idempotent creation with i18n support + cleanup of old-name shortcuts
  - `ShortcutManager.create_bat_file()` (line 138): Generates BAT wrappers with working_dir handling
  - `create_app_shortcut()` (line 420): Convenience wrapper
- **Usage example**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pyapps/matrix/utils/create_shortcut.py` (line 45)
- **AppUserModelID handler**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pycore/pyutils/common/appusermodelid.py` (imported by icon_generator, prevents duplicate taskbar icons)

### 2. **Linux systemd Service Creation (System Units)**
- **Main helper**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/common/debian_service_manager.sh`
  - `create_systemd_service()` (line 46): Generic systemd unit creator
    - Signature: `create_systemd_service(service_name, description, exec_command, working_dir, [user], [restart], [restart_sec], [cpu_limit], [memory_limit], [log_file])`
    - Auto-calculated CPU (default 20%) & memory limits by system RAM (≤2GB→200M, 2–4GB→300M, 4–8GB→500M, >8GB→1G)
    - Service file template with `CPUQuota`, `MemoryMax`, `MemoryHigh`, `TasksMax=100`, standardized `Restart=always`
  - `create_ncore_service()` (line 63): ncore-* naming wrapper
  - `remove_ncore_service()`, `check_service_status()`, `list_ncore_services()`, `update_service_resources()`
  - Automatically reloads systemd daemon after changes
  - **Idempotency**: stops before update, validates limits before apply

- **Distro-specific wrapper**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/common/pycore_service.sh`
  - Creates system unit for pycore daemon (entry point called by `pyservice.sh install`)
  - Handles `User=<real_user>` (finds desktop user via SUDO_USER/$USER fallback chain)

### 3. **Linux Desktop Entry (.desktop Files)**
- **Refresh utility**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/refresh_desktop_icons.sh`
  - Multi-DE detection (GNOME/KDE/XFCE via `pgrep`): lines 66–87
  - `update-desktop-database` (desktop entry cache refresh): line 21
  - `gtk-update-icon-cache` (icon cache): line 31
  - `update-mime-database` (MIME types): line 56
  - **Design intent**: .desktop files are manually written by new feature; this script refreshes caches post-creation

- **No existing .desktop generator** — Flutter app has template references (flutter_bloom/scripts), but core_node relies on custom creation

### 4. **Multi-Distro & Desktop Environment Detection**
- **Distro detection** (os-release sourcing):
  - `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/common/apt_repository_manager.sh` (lines 63–100+)
    - `get_real_login_user()`: Fallback chain: `SUDO_USER` → `ACTUAL_DESKTOP_USER` → first UID ≥1000
    - Normalizes Kali/Parrot → debian-base via `/etc/os-release` `ID_LIKE` field
    - Supports ubuntu, debian, kali base families
  - `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/common/install_cuda_toolkit.sh`
    - `cti_os_id()`: reads `/etc/os-release` ID field
    - Kali detection: `[ "$os" != "kali" ]` check (line with cuda-toolkit filter)

- **Desktop environment detection** (pgrep):
  - GNOME: `pgrep -x gnome-shell`
  - KDE: `pgrep -x plasmashell`
  - XFCE: `pgrep -x xfce4-panel`
  - Used in `refresh_desktop_icons.sh`; suggests DE-aware .desktop writers should follow pattern

- **sudo/rootless patterns**:
  - Core: `USE_SUDO` variable (set to `sudo` if not root, empty if root)
  - User detection: `SUDO_USER` → `$USER` → fallback to getent
  - Example in `13_ensure_python.sh` (line 141+): Uses `$USE_SUDO apt-get install`, real user determined for file permissions

### 5. **Python System Service (pycore_module_caller)**
- **Daemon launcher**: `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pyservice.sh`
  - **systemd subcommands** (lines 61–176 in help): `install`, `start`, `stop`, `restart`, `status`, `uninstall`
  - `install` delegates to `scripts/shells/linux/common/pycore_service.sh`
  - Unit file location: `/etc/systemd/system/pycore-module-caller.service` (created by debian_service_manager)
  - **No user units** (~/.config/systemd/user) — all system-wide via debian_service_manager
  - **DISPLAY forwarding** (lines ~150–170): Sets DISPLAY + DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS for AppIndicator3 (tray) to work with desktop user from systemd service

- **AppIndicator3 / Tray requirements**:
  - Linux: AppIndicator3 (system tray integration)
  - Windows: Qt tray (native)
  - Code: `pyservice.sh` comments indicate system DBUS needed for tray on Linux

### 6. **Design Intent (from docs)**
- **QiDongLian (Startup Chain)**:
  - Entry: `python pycore_module_caller.py` (unified, non-decomposable)
  - Layers: callmodule (config + scheduling) → pylauncher (singleton + service launch) → pythreadpool (threads)
  - Flow: config → ServiceLauncher instance → heartbeat + rpc_v2 (port 59000) + ui (WebView) + tray (Windows only) + register_event_handlers
  - Purpose: analyze startup chain for maintenance & debugging

- **PyLauncher intent** (from WindowLauncher docs):
  - Multi-window grid launcher utility (N terminals in R×C layout)
  - Exports: RatioCalculator, ScreenManager, ExplorerExecutor, ScriptGenerator, WindowsTerminalLauncher, EditorLauncher, ConfigManager, AppFinder, InteractiveMenu, WindowLauncher, main
  - Used by upper layers for concurrent startup orchestration

- **ServiceLauncher intent**:
  - Manages systemd/Windows Task Scheduler startup
  - Singleton pattern for pycore daemon
  - Registers event handlers for heartbeat + tray menu updates

### 7. **Idempotency Conventions**
- **Shortcut creation**: ShortcutManager checks existing → compares properties → only writes if changed
- **systemd service**: debian_service_manager stops service before update, re-enables, reloads daemon
- **Desktop refresh**: refresh_desktop_icons.sh is safe to run multiple times (only updates caches)
- **sudoers pattern**: Scripts use `$USE_SUDO` throughout; prefer mkdir/chown over forcing sudo

### Summary for Reuse
When building the new startup feature, reuse these:

1. **For Windows**: Import `ShortcutManager` from `pycore.pyutils.desktop.universal_shortcut`
   - Call `ensure_shortcut(name, command, icon_search_dir, working_dir, description, app_user_model_id)`
   - For Startup folder, write to `shell:startup` or use Registry Run key (not yet implemented; shortcut approach is current)

2. **For Linux**: Source `/scripts/shells/linux/common/debian_service_manager.sh`
   - Call `create_systemd_service()` for system units or wrap via `create_ncore_service()`
   - Distro/DE detection: use patterns from `apt_repository_manager.sh` + `refresh_desktop_icons.sh`
   - User detection: `get_real_login_user()` or `SUDO_USER` → `$USER` chain

3. **Desktop entry files** (.desktop for menu/launcher):
   - No generator exists; write manually (template in Flutter app or custom in new feature)
   - Post-write: call `update-desktop-database` + `gtk-update-icon-cache` + `update-mime-database`
   - Use `refresh_desktop_icons.sh` as reference for DE-aware refresh instructions

4. **Idempotency**: All helpers already built-in; check before write, use `ensure_*` not `create_*`

5. **Multi-distro**: Core_node targets debian/ubuntu/kali; use `ID_LIKE` from `/etc/os-release` for normalization

---

Tasks #7 (discovery) is now **complete**. Ready for #8–#11 implementation.
</teammate-message>

<teammate-message teammate_id="disc-ui" color="blue" summary="Complete pycore-manager settings flow analysis">
## End-to-End Settings Flow: Complete Analysis

### 1. EXAMPLE SETTING FLOW: `monitorClipboard` toggle

**Render & Toggle (PcSettingsPage.tsx:630-635):**
```typescript
{row(
  <Clipboard className="w-5 h-5" />, 'Monitor clipboard',
  'Watch the system clipboard and route captured content into pycore.',
  toggle(settings.monitorClipboard, false, () => patch({ monitorClipboard: !settings.monitorClipboard })),
  'bg-purple-500/10 text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400',
)}
```

**Component Pattern:**
- Toggle switch rendered via `toggle()` helper (lines 302-312) — returns a button with `onClick` handler
- `onClick` calls `patch()` with the inverted boolean
- `patch()` (lines 264-277) optimistically updates local state, then POSTs to backend
- No explicit "Save" button — changes persist immediately upon toggle

**Load on mount (lines 254-262):**
```typescript
useEffect(() => {
  loadSettings();  // Line 255
  // ...
}, [loadSettings, loadAssist, loadTtsTuning]);
```

`loadSettings()` (lines 121-138):
```typescript
const r = await pycoreApi.getSystemSettings();
const s = (r?.settings ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
setSettings({
  monitorClipboard: typeof s.monitorClipboard === 'boolean' ? s.monitorClipboard : DEFAULTS.monitorClipboard,
  // ... other fields
});
```

**Defaults (lines 32-44):**
```typescript
interface SystemSettings {
  monitorClipboard: boolean;
  scheduledScreenshot: boolean;
  screenshotInterval: number;
  notebooklmAutoConvert: boolean;
}

const DEFAULTS: SystemSettings = {
  monitorClipboard: false,
  scheduledScreenshot: false,
  screenshotInterval: 60,
  notebooklmAutoConvert: false,
};
```

---

### 2. API ENDPOINT & CLIENT METHOD

**Client Function (PycoreApi.ts:350-354):**
```typescript
getSystemSettings: () =>
  getJSON<SystemSettingsResponse>('/pyapi/api/local/user-data/system-settings'),
setSystemSettings: (settings: Record<string, unknown>) =>
  postJSON<{ success: boolean; error?: string }>(
    '/pyapi/api/local/user-data/system-settings', { settings }),
```

**HTTP Request Details:**
- **Method:** POST
- **Endpoint:** `/pyapi/api/local/user-data/system-settings`
- **Payload:** `{ "settings": { "monitorClipboard": true, "scheduledScreenshot": false, ... } }`
- **Response:** `{ "success": boolean, "error"?: string }`

**Target:** **PYCORE DIRECTLY** (not Laravel). The `/pyapi` prefix is a reverse proxy that routes to pycore on port 59000 (see constants.ts:24 `PYCORE_RPC_PORT = 59000`).

---

### 3. PERSISTENCE STORE

**Location:** Pycore's schemaless **user-data JSON store** (key-value sections)

**Backend Router (user_data_router.py:35-44):**
```python
@router.get("/system-settings", response_model=SystemSettingsResponse)
async def get_system_settings():
  return controller.get_system_settings()

@router.post("/system-settings", response_model=SystemSettingsResponse)
async def set_system_settings(request: SystemSettingsRequest):
  return controller.set_system_settings(request.settings)
```

**Backend Controller (user_data_controller.py:60-117):**
```python
SYSTEM_SETTINGS_SECTION = "system_settings"

def get_system_settings(self) -> SystemSettingsResponse:
  settings = self.store.get_section(SYSTEM_SETTINGS_SECTION)
  return SystemSettingsResponse(success=True, settings=settings or None)

def set_system_settings(self, settings: dict) -> SystemSettingsResponse:
  self.store.set_section(SYSTEM_SETTINGS_SECTION, settings)
  saved = self.store.get_section(SYSTEM_SETTINGS_SECTION)
  # Broadcast live to any connected UI
  try:
    THREAD_BUS.trigger_event("system_settings_update", {"settings": saved})
  except Exception as exc:
    ColorPrint.yellow(f"[UserData] settings broadcast failed: {exc}")
  return SystemSettingsResponse(success=True, settings=saved)
```

**Key Details:**
- Settings stored as **JSON object in pycore's user-data store** — NOT in a database
- Schemaless: any key can be added without migration
- **Broadcast-aware:** Changes trigger `system_settings_update` event via THREAD_BUS for live UI sync
- **No Laravel involvement:** Settings live entirely on pycore side

---

<teammate-message teammate_id="disc-launcher" color="cyan" summary="Terminal-grid launcher: full file:line analysis + cross-platform design">
## Terminal-Grid Launcher Analysis — Complete Findings

### 1. Grid Layout Computation & Core Module

**Function:** `WindowLauncher.calculate_window_layout()` 
**Location:** `pycore/pyutils/launcher/launcher.py:99-159`

**Layout Math (steps 1-3):**
- **Line 118-119:** Divide screen into grid cells: `target_window_width = screen_width // columns` and `target_window_height = screen_height // rows`
- **Line 121-122:** Reserve chrome (title bar 56px, horizontal padding 24px) and scale content to 0.78 to keep full window inside cell (avoids overlap)
- **Line 129-134:** Call `RatioCalculator.calculate_term_size()` to convert pixel dimensions → terminal character cells (cols × rows)
  - Uses calibration: char_width = 510px / 67 cols ≈ 7.6 px/col; char_height = 485px / 164 rows ≈ 3.0 px/row
- **Line 153-157:** Loop grid positions: `for row in range(rows): for col in range(columns):` and compute `(x, y)` pixels
  - Returns list: `[(x0,y0,c0,r0,w_px,h_px), ..., (xN,yN,cN,rN,w_px,h_px)]`

**Example output (4×3 grid on 3840×2160 screen):**
- Cell size: 960×720 px each
- After chrome reserve & scale: content ≈ 700×540 px
- Terminal size: 92 cols × 180 rows (per cell)
- All 12 windows positioned at `(col*960, row*720)`

---

### 2. Exact Call Chain: Launch 12 Terminals in 4×3 Grid

**Entry point:** `pycore/pyutils/launcher/launcher.py:530-661` (main function)

**Full execution path:**
```
main() [line 530]
  ↓ (interactive menu, then)
  ↓
config_manager.get_terminal_config() [line 630]  # reads grid_columns=4, grid_rows=3
  ↓
WindowLauncher(grid_columns=4, grid_rows=3, measured_columns=67, 
               measured_rows=164, measured_width_px=510, measured_height_px=485,
               calibration_actual_height=485, calibration_term_rows=270) [line 630-658]
  ↓
launcher.launch_windows() [line 661]
  ↓
ScreenManager.get_screen_dimensions() [line 189] → (x, y, width, height) via Win32 API
  ↓
calculate_ubuntu_count(total_windows=12) [line 195] → ubuntu_count=2 (reserve 2 for WSL)
  ↓
calculate_window_layout(screen_x, screen_y, screen_width, screen_height) [line 198]
  → returns [(x0,y0,92,180), (x1,y0,92,180), ..., (x11,y11,92,180)]
  ↓
wt_launcher.launch_windows(windows_config, delay=0.2, ubuntu_count=2) [line 204]
  ↓ (at wt_launcher.py:28-86)
  → split config: wt_windows=[0-9], ubuntu_windows=[10-11]
  ↓
for i, (x,y,cols,rows) in enumerate(wt_windows, 1):
  script_generator.create_wt_bat(i, x, y, cols, rows) [line 49]
  ↓ (10 .bat files created: launch_terminal_1.bat...launch_terminal_10.bat)
    Each contains: wt.exe -w new --pos "x,y" --size "cols.rows"
  ↓

  ↓ (via ExplorerExecutor, runs each .bat independently with 0.2s delay)
  ↓ (similar for ubuntu_windows: create_ubuntu_bat + execute [lines 79-84])
```

**Actual CLI command (per window):**
```bash

wt.exe -w new --pos "960,0" --size "92.180"
wt.exe -w new --pos "1920,0" --size "92.180"
# ... 10 total, then 2 more for Ubuntu
```

---

### 3. Windows-Specific vs OS-Agnostic

**WINDOWS-ONLY (hardcoded, no fallback):**
- `ScreenManager` (py/utils/launcher/screen_manager.py:34-110)
  - Lines 41-56: `ctypes.windll.user32.GetSystemMetrics(SM_XVIRTUALSCREEN)` — Win32 API only
  - Fallback to `EnumDisplayMonitors()` also Win32-only
  - **Linux equivalent:** `xrandr`, `wmctrl -m`, or `/proc/fb` — none available here

- `WindowsTerminalLauncher` (py/utils/launcher/wt_launcher.py:13-87)
  - Line 73: `wt.exe -w new --pos "x,y" --size "cols.rows"` — wt.exe is Windows Terminal (Windows 11+ shipped, older via Microsoft Store)
  - Line 83: Ubuntu launches also via `wt.exe` (WSL integration, not native Linux)

- `ScriptGenerator` (py/utils/launcher/script_generator.py:11-163)
  - Generates `.bat` files (Windows batch scripts)
  - Temp directory hardcoded: `C:\Users\{username}\.core_node\launch_multiple` (Windows only)

- `UbuntuFinder` (py/utils/launcher/ubuntu_finder.py:18-109)
  - Line 24: Searches `C:\Users\{username}\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu` for `.lnk` shortcuts
  - Win32 API: `win32com.client` to read shortcut properties
  - **Only works on Windows with WSL installed**

**OS-AGNOSTIC (pure logic, no platform deps):**
- `RatioCalculator` (py/utils/launcher/ratio_calculator.py:8-77) — arithmetic only
- `WindowLauncher.calculate_window_layout()` (py/utils/launcher/launcher.py:99-159) — grid math only
- `ConfigManager` (py/utils/launcher/config_manager.py) — reads JSON, platform-independent
- Configuration format (grid_columns, grid_rows, measurements, calibration) — works on any platform

---

### 4. Linux/Ubuntu Terminal Path (Current Reality)

**Docstring claim:** wt_launcher.py:1-4 says "Handles launching Windows Terminal windows **and Ubuntu terminals**"

**What actually happens:**
- Line 28: `ubuntu_count` parameter exists
- Line 43-44: Splits windows into `wt_windows` (first N-ubuntu_count) and `ubuntu_windows` (last ubuntu_count)
- Line 55-56: Calls `ubuntu_finder.get_first_ubuntu_shortcut()` — finds WSL launcher shortcut from Windows Start Menu
- Line 137-145: Generates `wt.exe ... wsl.exe` or `wt.exe ... <ubuntu_shortcut_target>`

**Verdict:**
- **Zero native Linux support.** The docstring's "Ubuntu terminals" is misleading — it actually means WSL (Windows Subsystem for Linux) running inside Windows Terminal.
- `ubuntu_count` reserves grid slots for WSL terminals, not real Linux terminal emulators.
- **No gnome-terminal, konsole, xfce4-terminal, xterm, qterminal, or tmux backend exists.**
- **No wmctrl/xdotool positioning code exists.**
- **Screen detection (ScreenManager) fails on Linux** — Win32 API calls will crash.

---

### 5. Command Inside Each Terminal

**No explicit command hardcoded.** Each window launches with:
- **Windows Terminal:** Default profile (PowerShell or cmd.exe, user's preference in Settings)
- **Ubuntu/WSL:** Target from `.lnk` shortcut (typically `ubuntu.exe` or `wsl.exe`) with no additional shell command

**If you need to run a specific command inside the terminal:**
- Modify the `.bat` file to include the command after the wt.exe call, OR
- Pass via Windows Terminal's profile configuration, OR
- Use `--title` and profile args (see WT_LAYOUT_REFERENCE.md line 20-50)

**Current limitation:** Grid launcher only handles window positioning and size — not initial command execution inside each terminal.

---

### 6. Recommended Abstraction Seam (Cross-Platform Backend Split)

**Current bottleneck:** `WindowsTerminalLauncher` is concrete to wt.exe; ScreenManager is Win32-only.

**Cleanest refactoring:**

```
pycore/pyutils/launcher/
├── launcher.py (unchanged — high-level logic)
├── screen_manager.py → platform_detector.py (new)
│   ├── ScreenManager (abstract)
│   ├── WindowsScreenManager (Win32 API)
│   └── LinuxScreenManager (xrandr / wmctrl)
├── terminal_launcher.py (new, abstract)
│   ├── TerminalLauncher (base class)
│   ├── WindowsTerminalLauncher (wt.exe)
│   └── LinuxTerminalLauncher (gnome-terminal / konsole / xfce4-terminal)
└── launcher.py main() [line 178]
    ↓
    if platform.system() == 'Windows':
        screen_mgr = WindowsScreenManager()
        term_launcher = WindowsTerminalLauncher()
    elif platform.system() == 'Linux':
        screen_mgr = LinuxScreenManager()
        term_launcher = LinuxTerminalLauncher()
    ↓ (rest of code unchanged)
```

**Interface contract (both must implement):**

```python
class TerminalLauncher:
    def launch_windows(self, windows_config, delay=0.2, ubuntu_count=0):
        """
        windows_config: [(x, y, cols, rows), ...]
        Returns: list of launched window handles/pids (for tracking)
        """
        raise NotImplementedError
```

**Linux implementation sketch:**
```python
class LinuxTerminalLauncher(TerminalLauncher):
    def launch_windows(self, windows_config, delay=0.2, ubuntu_count=0):
        desktop = detect_desktop_env()  # GNOME, KDE, XFCE, etc.
        
        if desktop == 'gnome':
            # gnome-terminal --geometry 120x40+0+0 &
            # xdotool for positioning after launch
        elif desktop == 'kde':
            # konsole -e bash &
            # wmctrl -i -r <window_id> -b add,maximized_vert,maximized_horz
        elif desktop == 'xfce':
            # xfce4-terminal --geometry 120x40+0+0 &
            # wmctrl for positioning
        # ... fallback to xterm
```

**Screen manager parallel:**
```python
class LinuxScreenManager(ScreenManager):
    def get_screen_dimensions(self):
        # xrandr --listmonitors (Wayland/X11)
        # or parse /sys/class/drm for KMS
        # Return: (screen_x, screen_y, screen_width, screen_height)
```

---

### Summary: Where to Inject Linux Backend

**Single entry point for platform detection:**
- `WindowLauncher.__init__()` [line 42-97] — after line 96, add:
  ```python
  if platform.system() == 'Windows':
      self.wt_launcher = WindowsTerminalLauncher(self.script_generator)
  elif platform.system() == 'Linux':
      self.wt_launcher = LinuxTerminalLauncher()
  else:
      self.wt_launcher = WindowsTerminalLauncher(...)  # fallback
  ```

- `ScreenManager.get_screen_dimensions()` [line 34] — make it a factory:
  ```python
  if platform.system() == 'Windows':
      return WindowsScreenManager().get_screen_dimensions()
  elif platform.system() == 'Linux':
      return LinuxScreenManager().get_screen_dimensions()
  ```

**No changes needed to:**
- `calculate_window_layout()` — platform-agnostic grid math
- `RatioCalculator` — pure arithmetic
- Main launcher flow — works once backends are swapped

This keeps the abstraction clean and minimal.

<teammate-message teammate_id="disc-pyservice" color="red" summary="pyservice mapping: subcommands, install behavior, config store, autostart paths">
## pyservice Entry Points & Autostart Mechanisms — Complete Mapping

### 1. Subcommands (pyservice.sh:184-189 / pyservice.ps1:224-289)

**Cross-platform subcommands:**
- `run` (default): Launch service with optional UI + prerequisites (both platforms)
- `config`: Headless cross-platform config CLI → `python -m pycore.pyutils.pyservice_cli config` (both platforms)
- `codesync`: Standalone Code Sync stdlib bootstrap (both platforms; systemd install on Linux only)

**Linux-only (systemd):**
- `install`: Create systemd unit at `/etc/systemd/system/pycore.service`, enable, start
- `start/stop/restart/status`: systemd unit control
- `uninstall`: Disable, remove unit, `systemctl daemon-reload`

**Windows behavior:** All systemd subcommands print notice → users must use Settings UI toggle instead.

---

### 2. Linux: `install` Creates systemd Unit (Complete Template)

**Delegate chain:** `pyservice.sh install` → `scripts/shells/linux/common/pycore_service.sh install` (line 256)

**Unit creation function:** `create_systemd_service()` from `debian_service_manager.sh`

**Generated unit** (`/etc/systemd/system/pycore.service`):
```ini
[Unit]
Description=Pycore Module Caller (headless)
After=network.target

[Service]
Type=simple
User=<DETECTED_REAL_USER>
WorkingDirectory=<REPO_ROOT>
ExecStart=/bin/bash <REPO_ROOT>/pyservice.sh run --no-ui --no-install
Restart=always
```

**Key lines in pycore_service.sh:**
- Service name: `pycore` (line 41)
- ExecStart template: line 46
- User detection: `pycore_resolve_user()` (lines 80-97) — tries `detect_system_user()` → `$SUDO_USER` → `whoami`
- Enable + start: `sudo systemctl enable pycore` + `sudo systemctl start pycore` (lines 145-146)
- Unit file write location: `/etc/systemd/system/${PYCORE_SERVICE_NAME}.service` (line 202)

**Sudo/privilege handling:**
- `$USE_SUDO` from `gvar_common.sh` (lines 72-77) — resolves to `sudo` if available, else empty
- All systemctl ops use `$USE_SUDO systemctl ...`

---

### 3. Windows Autostart: Shortcut + Fixed PS1 Launcher

**File:** `pycore/callmodule/platform/windows_startup_manager.py`

**Two-part design:**

**Part A: Fixed PowerShell launcher** (auto-regenerated on each `enable()` + every service start via `refresh()`):
- Path: `~/.core_node/data/autostart/PyCore_RPC_Server.ps1` (line 69)
- Content (pyservice mode, lines 124-138):
  ```powershell
  # PyCore RPC Server - auto-start launcher
  # AUTO-GENERATED: regenerated on every enable() and on every service start
  # (reflects current config).
  $ErrorActionPreference = 'SilentlyContinue'
  Set-Location -LiteralPath 'D:\programing\core_node'  # <repo root, quoted>
  & 'D:\programing\core_node\pyservice.ps1' -NoInstall
  ```
  Fallback (if `pyservice.ps1` missing): bare worker via `pythonw.exe` + `pycore_module_caller.py` (lines 142-150)

**Part B: Native shortcut (.lnk)** — points PowerShell at the fixed PS1:
- **Preferred location** (system-wide): `%PROGRAMDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\PyCore_RPC_Server.lnk` (lines 52-53)
- **Fallback** (if no admin): `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\PyCore_RPC_Server.lnk` (lines 59-62)
- Creation: via native Windows shell (WScript.Shell COM via pywin32, PowerShell fallback)
- "Enabled?" detection: shortcut existence (line 25)

---

### 4. Linux: XDG Autostart (.desktop) + Fixed Bash Launcher

**File:** `pycore/callmodule/platform/linux_startup_manager.py`

**Two-part design:**

**Part A: Fixed shell launcher** (auto-regenerated on each `enable()` + every service start via `refresh()`):
- Path: `~/.core_node/data/autostart/PyCore_RPC_Server.sh` (lines 52-53)
- Content (pyservice mode, lines 91-107):
  ```bash
  #!/usr/bin/env bash
  # PyCore RPC Server - auto-start launcher
  # AUTO-GENERATED: regenerated on every enable() and on every service start
  # (reflects current config).
  cd "/path/to/core_node" 2>/dev/null
  exec /usr/bin/env bash "/path/to/core_node/pyservice.sh" run --no-install
  ```
  Fallback (if `pyservice.sh` missing): bare worker via `python pycore_module_caller.py` (no UI)

**Part B: XDG .desktop entry** — compliant desktops run it on login:
- **Preferred** (system-wide, needs root): `/etc/xdg/autostart/pycore-rpc-server.desktop` (line 44)
- **Fallback** (per-user): `~/.config/autostart/pycore-rpc-server.desktop` (lines 45-46)
- Entry name: `pycore-rpc-server.desktop` (line 42)
- "Enabled?" detection: .desktop file existence (lines 79-80)

---

### 5. Config Store: pyservice_cli Reads/Writes (Critical for UI)

**File:** `pycore/pyutils/pyservice_cli/__main__.py`

**Design:** HTTP-first, file-fallback (lines 16-20):
- **Running service:** Changes via HTTP → apply live + broadcast to UI
- **Stopped service:** Persistent settings written to JSON files → take effect on next start
- **Runtime-only toggles:** `distribute`, `skip-update` require running service

#### System Settings (theme, lang, accent, etc.)

**Storage location:** `~/.core_node/config/user_data.json` — section `system_settings` (line 57 of pyservice.sh)
- **Resolved via:** `from pycore.pyfoundations.system_paths import get_user_data_store` (line 97)
- **Actual path resolution:** `get_app_config_dir() / "user_data.json"`
  - Windows: `C:\Users\{username}\.core_node\config\user_data.json`
  - Linux/WSL: `/var/_core_node/config/user_data.json` (or fallback to `~/.core_node/config/` if `/var` not writable)
- **Read:** `get_user_data_store().get_section("system_settings")` returns `dict` (line 98)
- **Write:** `get_user_data_store().update_section("system_settings", patch_dict)` (line 131)
- **Format:** JSON object, e.g.:
  ```json
  {
    "system_settings": {
      "theme": "dark",
      "lang": "zh",
      "accent": "blue",
      "blur": true
    }
  }
  ```

**CLI interface (lines 90-134):**
```bash
pyservice.sh config system get [--key THEME]
pyservice.sh config system set --key THEME --value dark
pyservice.sh config system set --json '{"theme":"dark","lang":"zh"}'
```

**HTTP endpoints:**
- `GET /api/local/user-data/system-settings` → `{"settings": {...}}` (line 92)
- `POST /api/local/user-data/system-settings` with body `{"settings": {...}}` (line 126)

#### Code Sync: Role + Peers (Persistent, Offline-capable)

**Storage location:** `pycore/pyutils/codesync/code_sync_peers.json` (committed)
- **Format:** JSON with structure:
  ```json
  {
    "peers": [
      {
        "host": "192.168.1.10",
        "id": "192.168.1.10:59000",
        "name": "lab",
        "port": 59000,
        "role": "dev|client"
      }
    ],
    "updated_at": <unix_timestamp>,
    "version": <number>
  }
  ```
- **Self/role:** Separate in config (read via `get_peer_config().get_role()`, line 173)

**CLI interface (lines 154-336):**
```bash
pyservice.sh config codesync show
pyservice.sh config codesync role [dev]
pyservice.sh config codesync peers list
pyservice.sh config codesync peers add --name lab --host 192.168.1.10 --role dev
pyservice.sh config codesync peers remove --id 192.168.1.10:59000
pyservice.sh config codesync distribute on|off          # running service only
pyservice.sh config codesync skip-update on|off          # running service only
```

**HTTP endpoints:**
- `GET /code-sync/peers` → `{"self": {...}, "peers": [...], "version": ...}` (line 155)
- `POST /code-sync/role` with body `{"role": "dev"|"client"}` (line 180)
- `POST /code-sync/peers/add|remove|update` (lines 215-250)
- `POST /code-sync/distribute` with body `{"enabled": true|false}` (line 267)
- `POST /code-sync/skip-update` with body `{"enabled": true|false}` (line 267)

---

### 6. pycore_module_caller.py Worker (Port 59000)

**File:** `pycore/pycore_module_caller.py:1-29`

**Purpose:**
- RPC v2 HTTP/WebSocket server on `:59000` (default, configurable)
- Singleton per machine (app_id = `pycore_module_caller`, port 59100 for detection)
- Launches pycore service threads (callmodule → pylauncher → pythreadpool)

**Launched by:**
1. **Preferred:** `pyservice.sh run` / `pyservice.ps1` — starts dashboard UI first (`poly_apps/pycore_laravel_wordflow_ui` on `:13054`), then worker
2. **Direct:** `python pycore/pycore_module_caller.py --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000` (etc.)
3. **Systemd unit:** `/bin/bash pyservice.sh run --no-ui --no-install` (headless)
4. **Autostart:** Fixed launcher scripts invoke `pyservice.sh run --no-install` / `pyservice.ps1 -NoInstall` (no prereq install)

**CLI args:** `--host`, `--port`, `--debug`, `--reload` (line 496-498 in pyservice.sh)

---

### 7. OS/Distro Detection

**Distro detection** (system_paths.py:75-105):
- Reads `/etc/os-release` → extracts `ID` (distro name) + `VERSION_ID` (version)
- Returns tuple: `(distro_name, version)` — e.g., `('ubuntu', '24')`, `('kali', '2026')`
- Distro names: ubuntu, debian, kali, fedora, etc.

**WSL detection** (system_paths.py:36-54):
- Checks `/mnt/c/Windows` + `/proc/version` for "Microsoft" / "WSL"

**Desktop Linux detection** (system_paths.py:57-72):
- Checks `$DISPLAY` / `$WAYLAND_DISPLAY` / `$DESKTOP_SESSION` / `$XDG_SESSION_TYPE`

---

### Critical Findings for UI Toggle Implementation

**What the toggle must do:**
1. **Call the startup manager API** (best practice — don't write config files):
   ```python
   from pycore.callmodule.platform.startup_manager import get_startup_manager
   manager = get_startup_manager()  # auto-selects Windows/Linux
   if toggle_enabled:
       manager.enable()   # creates .lnk / .desktop + regenerates launcher script
   else:
       manager.disable()  # removes .lnk / .desktop (script remains, harmless)
   ```

2. **Read current state** (no polling — managers detect at call time):
   ```python
   status = manager.get_status()  # contains: enabled, scope, location, entry_point, etc.
   ```

3. **File fallback** (if service down, no HTTP available):
   - Read/write system_settings directly: `get_user_data_store().get_section("system_settings")` + `.update_section(...)`
   - File is `~/.core_node/config/user_data.json`; format is JSON with sections as top-level keys

4. **Self-healing on boot** (already implemented):
   - Every service start calls `refresh_startup_launcher()` (in pycore_module_caller main)
   - Regenerates fixed launcher scripts so old versions auto-upgrade

---

**Config store (shared by UI toggle and CLI):** `~/.core_node/config/user_data.json` section `system_settings` (JSON dict); read/write via `get_user_data_store().get_section()` / `.update_section()`

### startup_manager factory + linux manager interface ###
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""
Cross-platform auto-start (boot/login) manager factory.

Returns the OS-native startup manager so each platform uses its own native
mechanism (Windows: a .lnk shortcut in the common Startup folder; Linux: an XDG
.desktop autostart entry). All managers share the same interface:
``is_enabled()``, ``enable()``, ``disable()``, ``toggle()``, ``get_status()``.
"""

import platform


class _UnsupportedStartupManager:
    """No-op manager for platforms without an implemented native mechanism."""

    def __init__(self, system: str):
        self._system = system or "unknown"

    def is_enabled(self) -> bool:
        return False

    def _unsupported(self) -> dict:
        return {"success": False, "enabled": False, "supported": False,
                "platform": self._system.lower(),
                "message": f"Auto-start is not supported on {self._system}."}

    def enable(self) -> dict:
        return self._unsupported()

    def disable(self) -> dict:
        return self._unsupported()

    def toggle(self) -> dict:
        return self._unsupported()

    def refresh(self) -> bool:
        return False

    def get_status(self) -> dict:
        return {"enabled": False, "supported": False,
                "platform": self._system.lower(),
                "message": f"Auto-start is not supported on {self._system}."}


def get_startup_manager(app_name: str = "PyCore_RPC_Server"):
    """Return the native startup manager for the current OS."""
    system = platform.system()
    if system == "Windows":
        from pycore.callmodule.platform.windows_startup_manager import WindowsStartupManager
        return WindowsStartupManager(app_name)
    if system == "Linux":
        from pycore.callmodule.platform.linux_startup_manager import LinuxStartupManager
        return LinuxStartupManager(app_name)
    return _UnsupportedStartupManager(system)

        ... [truncated 3067 chars]

<teammate-message teammate_id="disc-docs" color="yellow" summary="Complete cross-platform terminal grid research report with official sources">
## CROSS-PLATFORM TERMINAL GRID LAUNCHER: COMPLETE RESEARCH REPORT

### EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Launching N terminals in an R×C grid with autostart registration and idempotent shortcuts is **feasible on Linux (X11) but architecturally blocked under Wayland.** Windows offers mature, straightforward solutions. Below: authoritative decision matrix per OS/session type, each claim cited to official documentation.

---

## (1) LINUX TERMINAL EMULATORS — GEOMETRY FLAGS

| Terminal | Flag | Format | Supported? | Official Source |
|----------|------|--------|-----------|-----------------|
| **gnome-terminal** | `--geometry` | `WIDTHxHEIGHT+XOFFSET+YOFFSET` (chars/pixels) | X11 only | [Ubuntu manpage](https://manpages.ubuntu.com/manpages/xenial/man1/gnome-terminal.1.html) |
| **konsole** (KDE) | `--geometry` | `WIDTHxHEIGHT+XOFFSET+YOFFSET` | X11 + WM override | [KDE Community](https://kde-linux.kde.narkive.com/m2mFK8za/konsole-and-geometry) |
| **xfce4-terminal** | `--geometry` | `WIDTHxHEIGHT+XOFFSET+YOFFSET` | X11 + WM override | [Arch Linux manpage](https://man.archlinux.org/man/xfce4-terminal.1.en) |
| **qterminal** (Kali default) | **NONE** | Config file only (~/.config/qterminal.org/) | Config-based, not CLI | [Lubuntu Manual 26.04](https://manual.lubuntu.me/stable/3/3.1/3.1.2/qterminal.html) |
| **xterm** | `-geometry` (single dash) | `WIDTHxHEIGHT±XOFFSET±YOFFSET` | X11 only | [Oracle manpage](https://docs.oracle.com/cd/E88353_01/html/E37839/xterm-1.html) |

**Critical caveat:** `--geometry` is a *hint* to the window manager, not a guarantee. KWin (KDE) and other WMs may override or ignore it. For precise positioning, use WM-specific APIs (e.g., KDE's "Specific settings for this program" enforcement) or terminal multiplexers (see section 2).

---

## (2) X11 vs. WAYLAND — THE DEFINITIVE VERDICT

### **Can a client position its own windows under Wayland?**

**Official Answer: NO. This is architectural, not a limitation to work around.**

**From Wayland xdg-shell Protocol (Official):**
> "A deliberate design trait of Wayland makes application windows ignorant of their exact placement on screen or relative to other windows. This design choice prevents applications from arbitrarily positioning windows but allows the compositor greater flexibility in window management."

**Sources:**
- [Wayland xdg-shell Protocol (Wayland Explorer)](https://wayland.app/protocols/xdg-shell)
- [Canonical Mir Documentation: Window Positions Under Wayland](https://canonical-mir.readthedocs-hosted.com/stable/explanation/window-positions-under-wayland/)
- [Wayland Protocol Specification](https://wayland.freedesktop.org/docs/html/)

**The window geometry in Wayland:** Clients can specify `window_geometry` for *decoration-aware sizing* (e.g., drop shadows don't count toward window bounds), but **not for absolute positioning on-screen.** The compositor controls where windows appear.

---

### **wmctrl / xdotool Capabilities + Limits**

| Tool | X11 Support | Wayland Support | Why Not Portable | Official Status |
|------|------------|-----------------|------------------|-----------------|
| **wmctrl** | Full (EWMH) | Partial/broken (misses windows) | Depends on EWMH, X11-only standard | [EWMH spec](https://specifications.freedesktop.org/wm/latest-single/) |
| **xdotool** | Full (EWMH + XTest) | **NONE** | "Wayland eliminates everything xdotool can do" | [xdotool fragmentation analysis](https://www.semicomplete.com/blog/xdotool-and-exploring-wayland-fragmentation/) |
| **wlrctl** | No | wlroots-only (kdotool for KDE) | Compositor-specific; no standard | Community tool |

**Official verdict from xdotool analysis:** "On Wayland, each compositor requires its own unique implementation (kdotool for KDE, nothing for GNOME), making xdotool a dead-end for Wayland portability."

---

### **Portable Fallback for 4×3 Grid: Terminal Multiplexers**

Since Wayland forbids client-side positioning, arrange terminals *inside a single terminal window* using built-in multiplexer layouts:

#### **kitty (GPU-accelerated terminal)**
- **Grid layout:** Auto-calculated R×C based on # of windows
- **Syntax:** `enabled_layouts = tall,fat,grid,horizontal,vertical,splits,stack` (grid is one built-in)
- **Resizing:** Biased columns/rows via `biased_cols`, `biased_rows`
- **Portability:** Works X11 + Wayland
- **Source:** [kitty Layouts (Official)](https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/layouts/)
- **Limitation:** No parameter to force specific R×C; auto-calculates from window count

#### **tmux (Terminal Multiplexer)**
- **Tiled layout:** Spreads panes evenly via algorithm: `rows = columns = 1; while (rows * columns < n) { rows++; if (max_columns == 0 || columns < max_columns) columns++; }`
- **Configuration:** `set -g tiled-layout-max-columns 4` (limits to 4 cols; 0 = unlimited)
- **Portability:** X11 + Wayland (compositor-agnostic)
- **Session persistence:** Sessions survive logouts; independent of terminal process
- **Source:** [tmux Window Layouts (Official)](https://www.mintlify.com/tmux/tmux/advanced/layouts) | [tmux(1) man7.org](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/tmux.1.html)

---

### **Session Detection (X11 vs. Wayland)**

**Most reliable method (official):**
```bash
echo $XDG_SESSION_TYPE  # Output: "x11" or "wayland" (ground truth)
```

**Why not use $DISPLAY / $WAYLAND_DISPLAY?**
In Wayland sessions with XWayland enabled (typical for GNOME 50+), **both** `$DISPLAY` and `$WAYLAND_DISPLAY` are set. `$XDG_SESSION_TYPE` is the only reliable indicator.

**Source:** [nixCraft Session Detection Guide](https://www.cyberciti.biz/faq/howto-check-for-wayland-or-x11-with-my-linux-desktop/) | [Baeldung on Linux](https://www.baeldung.com/linux/display-server-xorg-wayland)

---

### **GNOME 50 Transition (Ubuntu 26.04 LTS)**

As of March 2026 (current):
- **GNOME 50 removed ~27,540 lines of X11 code** — no X11 backend
- **Ubuntu 26.04 LTS defaults to GNOME 50 + Wayland-only**
- **XWayland compatibility layer:** Runs X11 apps in a Wayland container, but **positioning still controlled by Wayland compositor**

**Sources:**
- [GNOME 50 X11 Drop (The Register)](https://www.theregister.com/2026/03/19/gnome-50-debuts-with-x11-axed-wayland-front-and-center/)
- [Ubuntu 26.04 Transition](https://thedailyperspective.org/article/2026-03-19-gnome-50-ends-27-years-of-x11-baggage-millions-of-ubuntu-users-to-follow-264060a2)

---

## (3) XDG DESKTOP AUTOSTART + PER-DE NOTES

### **Official Specification**

**Sources:**
- [Desktop Application Autostart Specification (freedesktop.org)](https://specifications.freedesktop.org/autostart-spec/autostart-spec-latest.html)
- [Desktop Entry Specification](https://eode.pages.freedesktop.org/xdg-specs/desktop-entry-spec/desktop-entry-spec-latest.html)

### **Idempotent .desktop Entry for Autostart**

```ini
[Desktop Entry]
Type=Application
Name=Terminal Grid Launcher
Exec=/path/to/launcher.sh
Hidden=false              # Idempotent toggle: set to true to disable
NoDisplay=false
TryExec=/path/to/launcher.sh  # WM skips if executable missing
X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=true  # GNOME extension (optional, defaults true)
OnlyShowIn=GNOME;KDE;XFCE      # Per-DE inclusion control
NotShowIn=Unity                 # Per-DE exclusion
```

**Location:** `~/.config/autostart/` (per-user) or `/etc/xdg/autostart/` (system-wide)

### **Idempotency via Hidden Key**

| Goal | Method | Reversible? |
|------|--------|-----------|
| Disable autostart | Set `Hidden=true` | Yes (set to false) |
| Enable autostart | Set `Hidden=false` (default) | Yes (set to true) |
| Delete permanently | `rm ~/.config/autostart/app.desktop` | No |

**Best practice:** Use `Hidden=true/false` to toggle; never delete .desktop files (re-run scripts may recreate them).

### **Per-Desktop Environment Notes**

- **GNOME:** Respects `Hidden=true`; also reads `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false` (non-standard)
- **KDE:** Respects `OnlyShowIn=KDE` and `Hidden=true`
- **XFCE:** Respects `Hidden=true` and `OnlyShowIn=XFCE`
- **TryExec:** All DEs skip .desktop if `TryExec` binary is missing (robust for conditional autostart)

---

## (4) SYSTEMD USER SERVICES vs. DESKTOP AUTOSTART

### **When to Use Which**

| Mechanism | Use Case | Scope | Persistence |
|-----------|----------|-------|-------------|
| **.desktop autostart** | GUI apps, user-initiated | User *session* (within DE) | Starts at DE login; stops at logout |
| **systemd --user** | Long-running services, daemons | User *instance* (no DE needed) | Can run at boot w/ linger; survives logouts |
| **systemd system** | System-wide services, root-owned | Entire system | Runs at boot for all users |

### **Official Documentation**

**Source:** [loginctl official manual (freedesktop.org)](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/loginctl.html)

### **enable-linger: Boot Without Login**

```bash
loginctl enable-linger $USER  # Spawns user systemd at boot; persists after logout
```

**What it does:** Normally, the systemd user instance starts at first login and stops at last logout. With linger enabled, it starts at boot and never stops (unless explicitly killed).

**Use cases:**
- Background services that should run 24/7
- Terminal multiplexer sessions (tmux) that persist across reboots

### **User Service File Pattern**

**Location:** `~/.config/systemd/user/launcher.service`

```ini
[Unit]
Description=Terminal Grid Launcher Service
After=graphical-session-pre.target

[Service]
Type=simple
ExecStart=/path/to/launcher.sh
Restart=on-failure

[Install]
WantedBy=graphical-session.target  # Starts with GNOME/KDE/XFCE session
```

**Enable at boot:**
```bash
systemctl --user enable launcher.service
loginctl enable-linger $USER
```

### **Key Difference**

- `.desktop autostart`: Runs *during* the DE session (GNOME/KDE/XFCE starts it)
- `systemd --user`: Runs *independently* of DE; persists after logout w/ linger

**Recommendation:** For "launch terminals at user login," use **.desktop autostart** (simpler, DE-managed). For background services, use **systemd --user**.

---

## (5) WINDOWS AUTOSTART MECHANISMS

### **Three Official Methods**

**Source:** [Microsoft Learn: schtasks /create](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-create)

#### **1. Startup Folder (Simplest)**
- **Path:** `shell:startup` → `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup`
- **Method:** Copy `.lnk` shortcut file here
- **Idempotency:** Check if file exists before copy
- **Scope:** Current user only
- **GUI-friendly:** Yes; drag-and-drop supported

#### **2. Registry Run Key (Per-user, Command-line)**
- **Path:** `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run`
- **Command:** `reg add HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run /v "MyApp" /d "C:\path\launcher.exe" /f`
- **Idempotency:** `/f` flag overwrites existing value silently
- **Scope:** Current user only
- **Logging:** No built-in logging

#### **3. Task Scheduler (Most Robust)**
- **Command:** `schtasks /create /tn "Terminal Grid Launcher" /tr "C:\path\launcher.exe" /sc onlogon /f`
- **Idempotency:** `/f` flag replaces existing task by name (no confirmation)
- **Scope:** Per-user or system-wide (`/ru system`)
- **Features:** Logging, conditional execution (ONIDLE, ONSTART, ONEVENT), delayed start, run-as permissions
- **Recommended:** Yes, for production use

**Official parameter guide:** [schtasks /create (Microsoft Learn)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-create)

---

### **Desktop Shortcuts (.lnk) via PowerShell**

**Official method: WScript.Shell COM**

**Source:** [Microsoft Learn: Create desktop shortcut with WSH](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/troubleshoot/windows-client/admin-development/create-desktop-shortcut-with-wsh)

```powershell
$WshShell = New-Object -ComObject WScript.Shell
$ShortcutPath = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\Terminal Grid.lnk"
$Shortcut = $WshShell.CreateShortcut($ShortcutPath)
$Shortcut.TargetPath = "C:\Windows\System32\cmd.exe"
$Shortcut.Arguments = "/c launcher.exe"
$Shortcut.WorkingDirectory = "C:\path\to\launcher"
$Shortcut.IconLocation = "C:\path\to\icon.ico"
$Shortcut.Save()
```

**Idempotent pattern:**
```powershell
if (-not (Test-Path $ShortcutPath)) {
    # Create shortcut (avoid re-creating if exists)
} else {
    # Option A: Skip if exists
    # Option B: Delete and recreate to ensure fresh copy
    Remove-Item $ShortcutPath -Force
    # ... create new shortcut
}
```

---

## (6) IDEMPOTENCY BEST PRACTICES (CROSS-PLATFORM)

### **Linux Desktop Entries**

| Goal | Method | Idempotent? | Reversible? |
|------|--------|------------|-----------|
| Disable autostart | Set `Hidden=true` | ✓ Yes | ✓ Yes (set to false) |
| Enable autostart | Set `Hidden=false` | ✓ Yes | ✓ Yes (set to true) |
| Delete entry | `rm ~/.config/autostart/app.desktop` | ✗ No (re-run copies file) | ✗ No |

**Best practice:** Use Hidden key; never delete .desktop files from version control.

**Source:** [Desktop Autostart Spec](https://specifications.freedesktop.org/autostart-spec/autostart-spec-latest.html)

---

### **Linux Systemd User Services**

**Idempotent enable/disable:**
```bash
# Enable (idempotent):
systemctl --user enable launcher.service
loginctl enable-linger $USER

# Disable (idempotent):
systemctl --user disable launcher.service
# Note: loginctl disable-linger is irreversible if no other services use it
```

**Source:** [loginctl manual](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/loginctl.html)

---

### **Windows Task Scheduler (schtasks)**

**Idempotent create + update:**
```powershell
schtasks /create /tn "MyTask" /tr "C:\launcher.exe" /sc onlogon /f
```

The `/f` flag **replaces the task by name if it exists**, ensuring idempotency across multiple runs.

**Idempotent disable:**
```powershell
schtasks /change /tn "MyTask" /disable
```

**Source:** [schtasks reference (ss64.com)](https://ss64.com/nt/schtasks.html) + [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-create)

---

### **Windows Registry (reg add)**

**Idempotent add + overwrite:**
```powershell
reg add HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run /v "MyApp" /d "C:\launcher.exe" /f
```

The `/f` flag forces overwrite without confirmation.

**Source:** Windows Command Reference (built-in via `reg /?`)

---

### **Windows Shortcuts (PowerShell)**

**Idempotent creation:**
```powershell
$ShortcutPath = "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\Terminal Grid.lnk"
if (Test-Path $ShortcutPath) {
    Remove-Item $ShortcutPath -Force
}
# Create shortcut
```

Alternatively, use `-Force` on copy: `Copy-Item $SourceShortcut $DestShortcut -Force` (overwrites silently).

**Source:** [PowerShell New-Item & Test-Path patterns](https://www.commandinline.com/powershell-new-item-test-path/)

---

## DECISION MATRIX: RECOMMENDED APPROACH PER OS/SESSION

### **Linux — Ubuntu 24.04 (GNOME/KDE/XFCE + X11 available)**

| Requirement | X11 Session | Wayland Session |
|-------------|-------------|-----------------|
| **Grid layout** | `--geometry` on gnome-terminal/konsole/xfce4-terminal; honor WM override | Use kitty/tmux inside single terminal (Wayland won't position) |
| **Autostart** | .desktop file in `~/.config/autostart/` | Same .desktop file (Wayland-compatible) |
| **Service registration** | .desktop autostart (simpler) | .desktop autostart or systemd --user |
| **Boot persistence** | systemd --user + linger if needed | systemd --user + linger if needed |
| **Session detection** | `if [ "$XDG_SESSION_TYPE" = "x11" ]; then ...` | Use multiplexer layout as fallback |

---

### **Linux — Kali (XFCE + qterminal)**

- **Default terminal:** qterminal (no `--geometry` flag)
- **Issue:** Position/size lost on reopen; config-based only
- **Recommendation:** Use xfce4-terminal (supports `--geometry`) or switch to kitty for X11 + Wayland portability

**Source:** [Lubuntu Manual qterminal](https://manual.lubuntu.me/stable/3/3.1/3.1.2/qterminal.html)

---

### **Linux — Ubuntu 26.04 LTS (GNOME 50 + Wayland Mandatory)**

| Requirement | Method | Why |
|-------------|--------|-----|
| **Grid 4×3** | kitty/tmux layout (not WM positioning) | Wayland forbids client positioning (architectural) |
| **Autostart** | `~/.config/autostart/launcher.desktop` | GNOME 50 supports .desktop |
| **Session type** | Detect `XDG_SESSION_TYPE=wayland`; branch to multiplexer | Mandatory Wayland |
| **Boot persistence** | systemd --user (optional linger for services) | .desktop alone sufficient for session autostart |

---

### **Windows (10/11)**

| Requirement | Recommended | Idempotency | Complexity |
|-------------|-------------|------------|-----------|
| **Startup on logon** | Task Scheduler: `schtasks /create /sc onlogon /f` | ✓ Yes (`/f` overwrites) | Low (CLI one-liner) |
| **Desktop shortcut** | PowerShell WScript.Shell + `Test-Path` | ✓ Yes (check before create) | Low |
| **Disable startup** | `schtasks /change /tn "MyTask" /disable` | ✓ Yes | Low |
| **Registry autostart** | `reg add HKCU\...\Run /v "MyApp" /d "path" /f` | ✓ Yes (`/f` overwrites) | Very low |

**Best practice:** Use Task Scheduler for robustness (logging, granular control, error reporting). Registry for simple "run at logon" with no extra features.

---

## WHAT IS IMPOSSIBLE vs. POSSIBLE

### **Impossible (Per Official Design)**
- **Precise client-side window positioning under Wayland** — Wayland architecture forbids this entirely; not a limitation to bypass
- **xdotool / wmctrl on Wayland** — X11-only tools; no cross-compositor standard equivalent
- **qterminal geometry flag** — Not implemented; config-based only (loses on reopen)

### **Possible & Portable**
- **X11 sessions:** `--geometry` flags + wmctrl/xdotool (limited by WM enforcement)
- **Wayland + X11 (hybrid):** Terminal multiplexer grid inside single window (kitty/tmux)
- **Autostart (.desktop):** Works identically X11 + Wayland; toggle via `Hidden=true/false`
- **Windows:** Task Scheduler `/f` flag is true idempotent; WScript.Shell + Test-Path for shortcuts

---

172:    def calculate_ubuntu_count(self, total_windows):
    def calculate_ubuntu_count(self, total_windows):
        """
        Calculate number of Ubuntu terminals to launch
        
        Args:
            total_windows: Total number of windows
        
        Returns:
            int: Number of Ubuntu terminals (at least 2, 4 if 16 windows)
        """
        if total_windows >= 16:
            return 4
        elif total_windows >= 2:
            return 2
        else:
            return 0

1	# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
2	"""
3	Linux Terminal Launcher
4	Launches a positioned grid of native terminals on Linux (X11 / Wayland).
5	
6	This is the Linux counterpart of ``wt_launcher.WindowsTerminalLauncher`` and
7	exposes the same public surface (``launch_windows``). It picks one of two
8	strategies based on the session type:
9	
10	  * X11 -- geometry-capable emulators (xfce4-terminal / gnome-terminal /
11	    konsole / xterm) accept an X11 ``--geometry`` hint, so we launch N separate,
12	    individually-positioned windows. Whether or not the emulator honoured the
13	    hint, we then best-effort enforce position by PID using ``wmctrl`` or
14	    ``xdotool`` (whichever exists) -- geometry is only a hint the WM may ignore.
15	
16	  * Wayland -- a real client-positioned multi-window grid is IMPOSSIBLE by
17	    design: the xdg-shell protocol deliberately forbids a client from setting
18	    its own window's screen coordinates (the compositor owns placement). So we
19	    fall back to a SINGLE window whose internal PANES form the grid, using
20	    ``kitty`` (session file) or ``tmux`` (split panes), or, failing those, N
21	    plain unpositioned terminals that the compositor tiles on its own.
22	
23	Note: ``qterminal`` (Kali's default) has no geometry flag, so it is excluded
24	from the geometry-emulator list used by the separate-window X11 path. It IS,
25	however, used for the tmux-attach and unpositioned fallbacks (it can run a
26	command via ``-e``). Every launch is wrapped in try/except; this class never
27	raises and returns a best-effort list of launched PIDs.
28	"""
29	
30	import math
31	import os
32	import shutil
33	import subprocess
34	import tempfile
35	import time
36	
37	
38	class LinuxTerminalLauncher:
39	    """Launch a positioned grid of native Linux terminals (X11 / Wayland)."""

<teammate-message teammate_id="build-linux-term" color="purple" summary="Both X11 refinements done, py_compile clean">
Both refinements applied to /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/pycore/pyutils/launcher/linux_terminal_launcher.py. Both files py_compile clean (linux_screen_manager.py, linux_terminal_launcher.py). No other files touched, no import of pycore, no commit.

(1) X11 position enforcement now supports wmctrl AND xdotool.
- New `_enforce_positions(placements)`: prefers wmctrl if present, else xdotool; if neither, prints a note and leaves WM placement. 0.4s settle before addressing windows. Never raises.
- `_launch_x11_grid` now records (title, pid, x, y) per launched window so either tool can address it (wmctrl by title, xdotool by pid).
- New `_move_with_wmctrl(title, x, y)`: `wmctrl -r <title> -e 0,<x>,<y>,-1,-1` (move only, -1 keeps size).
- New `_move_with_xdotool(pid, x, y, width=None, height=None)`: `xdotool search --sync --pid <pid>` -> for each returned window id `xdotool windowmove <wid> <x> <y>`, plus `xdotool windowsize <wid> <w> <h>` only when a pixel size is passed (currently move-only, since the grid carries char cells not pixels; the width/height params are wired and ready if you later pass pixel size).

(2) tmux-attach and unpositioned fallbacks now use a BROAD emulator list INCLUDING qterminal.
- New class const `FALLBACK_EMULATORS = ("xfce4-terminal","gnome-terminal","konsole","qterminal","xterm")` and helper `_find_fallback_emulator()` (first on PATH; defaults to "xterm").
- `_launch_tmux` and `_launch_plain` now call `_find_fallback_emulator()` instead of the xterm-only fallback. `_build_attach_argv` documents qterminal under the shared `-e <cmd>` convention.
- `X11_EMULATORS` (geometry-only, qterminal excluded) is unchanged and still gates the separate-window path.

Behavior on the real Kali box (X11, only qterminal/tmux/xdotool/xdpyinfo): no geometry-capable emulator -> falls through to `_launch_paned_grid` -> no kitty -> tmux: builds a `pylauncher` session with N tiled panes, then opens it via `_find_fallback_emulator()` = qterminal (`qterminal -e tmux attach -t pylauncher`). Result is a single qterminal window holding a real R×C grid of PANES (e.g. 12 panes for 4x3). The separate-window X11 path (with wmctrl/xdotool enforcement) is preserved for boxes that DO have a geometry emulator.

One decision I want to flag: I kept the separate-window-vs-paned gate on "is a geometry-capable emulator present", so Kali deterministically lands on the tmux paned grid you specified. I did NOT make it launch separate qterminal windows positioned purely by xdotool-by-PID, because (a) your concrete Kali expectation was the tmux paned grid, and (b) qterminal can't set its initial char-cell size, so a PID-only path would give unsized, overlapping windows until moved. If you'd rather have separate positioned qterminal windows on Kali instead of the paned grid, say so and I'll add that branch (would need pixel width/height per cell to also drive xdotool windowsize).

Decision logic in launch_windows() (exactly as you specced):
- is_wayland = XDG_SESSION_TYPE == 'wayland'
- positioner = _find_positioner()  (wmctrl > xdotool > None)
- geom_emu = _find_x11_emulator()  (geometry list, qterminal excluded)
- any_emu  = _find_fallback_emulator_or_none()  (broad list incl. qterminal; None if PATH empty)
- if not wayland and positioner and any_emu -> _launch_x11_positioned(configs, geom_emu or any_emu, positioner, delay)
- elif not wayland and geom_emu -> existing _launch_x11_grid (geometry-hint-only)
- else -> existing _launch_paned_grid (kitty/tmux/_launch_plain intact)

_launch_x11_positioned(configs, emulator, positioner, delay):
- Derives per-cell PIXEL size from grid spacing via _cell_pixel_size(): sorts distinct x-offsets, cell_w = xs[1]-xs[0] (>=2 distinct), same for y -> cell_h; else None.
- Per window, title=f"pylauncher-{i}", inner = "printf '\\033]0;{title}\\007'; exec {self.command or '$SHELL'}" run via bash -lc. The OSC-0 self-title is what makes title-matching work with qterminal (no --title flag).
- argv via _build_titled_argv(): geometry-capable emus also get their --geometry hint; command convention per emu (xfce4: --command=bash -lc '<inner>' with proper '\'' escaping; gnome: -- bash -lc <inner>; konsole/qterminal/xterm: -e bash -lc <inner>). Popen(start_new_session=True), record (title,x,y), sleep(delay).
- Settle 0.5s, then enforce per window: wmctrl -> `wmctrl -r <title> -e 0,<x>,<y>,<cell_w or -1>,<cell_h or -1>`; xdotool -> `xdotool search --sync --name <title> windowmove <x> <y>` plus, if cell_w, `xdotool search --name <title> windowsize <cell_w> <cell_h>`. Each wrapped in try/except, one status line per window. Returns pids.

New methods: _launch_x11_positioned, _build_titled_argv, _place_by_title_wmctrl (static), _place_by_title_xdotool (static), _find_positioner (static), _find_fallback_emulator_or_none, _cell_pixel_size (static). Existing _launch_x11_grid / _enforce_positions / _move_with_* / _build_x11_argv (geometry path) and all kitty/tmux/plain fallbacks unchanged.

    def _launch_x11_positioned(self, configs, emulator, positioner, delay):
        """
        Launch N separate windows and position each by its window TITLE.

        Unlike ``_launch_x11_grid`` this does not depend on the emulator having a
        ``--geometry`` flag: each window self-sets a unique title via an OSC
        escape sequence, then a positioner (wmctrl or xdotool) moves/sizes the
        window matched by that title. This is what lets qterminal (Kali's only
        emulator, no geometry flag) form a real grid of separate windows.

        Args:
            configs: List of 4-tuples (x, y, cols, rows).
            emulator: Emulator to launch (geometry-capable preferred, else any).
            positioner: 'wmctrl' or 'xdotool'.
            delay: Delay between launches in seconds.

        Returns:
            list: Launched PIDs.
        """
        geom_capable = emulator in self.X11_EMULATORS
        cell_w, cell_h = self._cell_pixel_size(configs)
        print(f"X11 session: launching {len(configs)} separate '{emulator}' "
              f"window(s), positioned by title via {positioner}"
              + (f" (cell {cell_w}x{cell_h}px)" if cell_w else "") + ".")

        pids = []
        placements = []  # (title, x, y) -- matched later by title

        for i, (x, y, cols, rows) in enumerate(configs, 1):
            title = f"pylauncher-{i}"
            # Inner command: self-set the window title (OSC 0) so the positioner
            # can find this window, then run the target command (or login shell).
            target = self.command or "${SHELL:-bash}"
            inner = "printf '\\033]0;%s\\007'; exec %s" % (title, target)
            # A geometry hint gets the window roughly placed before we enforce;
            # harmless on emulators that ignore it.
            geometry = f"{cols}x{rows}+{x}+{y}" if geom_capable else None
            argv = self._build_titled_argv(emulator, inner, geometry)
            if argv is None:
    
        ... [truncated 2321 chars]

Gating in launch_windows() (your form):

_launch_x11_positioned(configs, emulator, positioner, delay):
A. _cell_pixel_size(configs): xs=sorted(set x), ys=sorted(set y); cell_w=xs[1]-xs[0] if >=2 distinct else None; cell_h likewise. (No gap subtraction; easy to add if you want an 8px gutter.) None -> move-only.
B. Per window i, title=f"pylauncher-{i}", inner="printf '\\033]0;%s\\007'; exec %s" % (title, self.command or '${SHELL:-bash}') run via bash -lc -- OSC-0 self-title is what lets qterminal be addressed by title. argv via _build_titled_argv(): xfce4-terminal -> ["xfce4-terminal", "--command=bash -lc "+shlex.quote(inner)]; gnome-terminal -> [..., "--","bash","-lc",inner]; konsole/qterminal/xterm -> [emu,"-e","bash","-lc",inner]. Geometry-capable emus also get their --geometry "{cols}x{rows}+{x}+{y}" hint. Popen(start_new_session=True); record (title,x,y); sleep(delay).
C. After ~0.5s settle, enforce by TITLE: wmctrl -> `wmctrl -r <title> -e 0,<x>,<y>,<cell_w or -1>,<cell_h or -1>`; xdotool -> wid via `xdotool search --sync --name <title>`, then per wid `windowmove <wid> <x> <y>` and, if cell_w, `windowsize <wid> <cell_w> <cell_h>`. try/except each, one status line per window. Returns pids.

7:**Git & no-revert (mandatory):** Run **`git commit` ONLY** (plus read-only `git status`/`log`/`diff`). NEVER run any other Git operation — no `checkout`/`switch`/`reset`/`revert`/`restore`/`clean`/`rm`/`mv`/`stash`/`rebase`/`merge`/`cherry-pick`/`branch -D`/`push`/`pull`/`fetch --prune` or any `--force`. Record renames/removals via normal file tools + the next commit, never `git rm`/`git mv`/`git reset`. NEVER revert, undo, roll back, overwrite, or delete existing code or the user's local (uncommitted) changes — only move forward (add/extend/fix in place); if something looks wrong, surface it and ask rather than reverting. Commit completed work locally; never push/sync unless explicitly asked. Full rule: [development-guides/GIT_AND_NO_REVERT_SAFETY.md](development-guides/GIT_AND_NO_REVERT_SAFETY.md).

13	**Status: enforced.** This is the canonical conflict-resolution rules document. It
14	exists because repeated reverts / destructive Git operations and merge "resolutions"
15	that discarded local work wiped the user's in-progress changes. It must never happen

18	
19	## 1. Git: `git commit` ONLY
20	
21	An AI agent may run **`git commit`** plus read-only inspection (`git status` /
22	`git log` / `git diff`). It MUST NOT run any other Git operation, including:
23	`checkout`, `switch`, `reset`, `revert`, `restore`, `clean`, `rm`, `mv`, `stash`,
24	`rebase`, `merge`, `cherry-pick`, `branch -D`, `tag -d`, `push`, `pull`, `fetch`,
25	`fetch --prune`, `gc --prune`, `reflog expire`, or any `--force` variant.
26	
27	- Renames/removals of tracked files: do them with the normal filesystem tools the
28	  task requires and let the next `git commit` record them — never via `git rm` /
29	  `git mv` / `git reset`.
30	- **Never push, pull, fetch, or sync to a remote** — not even when explicitly asked
31	  in passing. Commits stay local; publishing is the user's decision and is performed
32	  outside the agent.
33
